### T061fc06d787d17ceb28a4001

Text hash 2b0b961f5170990963d4a3c952d1382d9c00f9c017febb11f135c91f74660a3d; absolute characters 0–680; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8252-exemption-samuel-j-gompers-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt

```text
Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Samuel J. Gompers, Chief Clerk, Department of Labor, who was exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year by Executive Order No. 7958 of August 19, 1938, be further exempted from such compulsory retirement for a period of one year:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby further exempt the said Samuel J. Gompers from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending September 30, 1940.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

September 14, 1939.

```

### T0a5e8eaeb56006659dd06026

Text hash 972f75a7914122bf30875b90ba6e30600fa4d57903db78a68cfaa1ebbea471b4; absolute characters 0–852; files: jobs/mabillon-held-printings/output/texts/EO_7949.txt

```text
~

Skp 2


boc]

Nn —

€xecutive Order

EXEMPTION OF STEPHEN W. HAMILTON From ComMPuLsoRY RETIREMENT
FoR AGE

WHEREAS, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Stephen W.
Hamilton, deputy collector of customs, Treasury Department, who, during the cur-
rent month, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the
service, applicable to him, be exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a

period of one year:

NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me
by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S. C., title 5, sec.
715a), I hereby exempt the said Stephen W. Hamilton from compulsory retirement
for age for a period of one year ending August 31, 1939.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT

Tue Waite Hovss,
August 9, 1988.

[No. 7949]

U.S. GOVERNMENT PRINTING OFFICE: 1938

```

### T4611d40206f5918916cb6867

```text
DEPOSITED BY THE
UNITED STATES OF AMERICA

JUL 15 1938


Executive Order

EXEMPTION OF Davip A. Bropir From ComMpuLsorRy RETIREMENT FOR
AGE

WHEREAS, in my judgment, the public interest requires that David A. Brodie,
farm superintendent, St. Elizabeths Hospital, Department of the Interior, who during
the month of July, 1938, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separa-
tion from the service, applicable to him, be exempted from compulsory retirement for
age for a period of one year:

NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me
by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S. C., title 5, sec. 715a),
LI hereby exempt the said David A. Brodie from compulsory retirement for age for a

period of one year ending July 31, 1939.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT
Tae Waite Hovse,
June 16, 1988.

[No. 7914]

U.S SOVERNMENT PRINTING OFFICE. 1938

```

```text

Executive @rder

EXEMPTION OF ULysses S. SHEWMAKER FROM CoMPULSORY RETIREMENT
FOR AGE

WHEREAS, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Ulysses S.
Shewmaker, administrative assistant in the Publications Section of the Division of
Bookkeeping and Warrants, Treasury Department, who during the month of Septem-
ber 1938, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the
service, applicable to him, be exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period
of one year:

NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me
by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S. C., title 5, sec. 715a),
I hereby exempt the said Ulysses S. Shewmaker from compulsory retirement for age
for a period of one year ending September 30, 1939.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT
Tue Waite Hovss,
July 14, 1988.

[No. 7932]

©. S$. GOVERNMENT PRINTING OFFICE: 1938

```

```text
September 18, 1940.
```

```text
EXEMPTION OF ALFRED R. GouLp From Computsory RETIREMENT

WHEREAS, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Alfred R. Gould,
artistic operator of geometric lathe, Bureau of Engraving and Printing, Treasury
Department, who during the month of October, 1938, will reach the retirement age
prescribed for automatic separation froin the service, applicable to him, be exempted
from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year:

NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me
by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S. C., title 5, sec. .
715a), I hereby exempt the said Alfred R. Gould from compulsory retirement for
age for a period of one year ending October 31, 1939.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT
Toe Waite Hovsz,
September 16, 19388.

{[No. 7974]

U. S. GOVERNMENT PRINTING OFFICE: 1938
```

```text
Executive Order

EXEMPTION OF SAMUEL J. GompERS FROM COMPULSORY RETIREMENT

WHEREAS, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Samuel J. Gom-
pers, Chief Clerk, Department of Labor, who, during the month of September, 1938,
will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service,
applicable to him, be exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of
one year:

by section 204 of the Act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S. C., title 5, sec.
715a), I hereby exempt the said Samuel J. Gompers from compulsory retirement for
age for a period of one year ending September 30, 1939.
```

```text
Executive Order

EXEMPTION OF Marion C. HarGrove From ComMputsory RETIREMENT
FOR AGE

WHEREAS, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Marion C. Har-
grove, purchasing officer, Government of the District of Columbia, who during the
month of November 1938, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic
separation from the service, applicable to him, be exempted from compulsory retire-
ment for age for a period of one year:

by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S. C., title 5, sec. 715a),
I hereby exempt the said Marion C. Hargrove from compulsory retirement for age
for a period of one year ending November 30, 1939.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT
Tue Wuite Hovse,
July 14, 1938.

[No. 7931]
```

```text
FurRTHER EXEMPTION OF JoHN H. Tuomas From CoMPULSORY

WHEREAS, in my judgment, the public interest requires that John H. Thomas,
Assistant Solicitor and member of the Board of Appeals, Department of the Interior,
who was exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period one year ending

by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S. C., title 5, sec. 715a),
I hereby further exempt the said John H. Thomas from compulsory retirement for
age for a period of one year ending September 30, 1939.
```

```text
me by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S. C., title 5, sec.
715a), I hereby exempt the said Harry Peale from compulsory retirement for age

for a period of six months ending July 31, 1939.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT
Tue Waite Hovsz,
November 7, 1938.

[No. 8002]

U.S. GOVERNMENT PRINTING OFFICE: 1938

```

```text
AG

Executive Order

EXEMPTION OF FRANK S. SmitH From CoMPuLsoRY RETIREMENT FOR AGE

WHEREAS, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Frank S. Smith,
special agent of the Federal Bureau of Investigation, Department of Justice, who
during the month of September, 1938, will reach the retirement age prescribed for
automatic separation from the service, applicable to him, be exempted from com-
pulsory retirement for age for a period of one year:

NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me
by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S. C., title 5, sec. 7158),
I hereby exempt the said Frank S. Smith from compulsory retirement for age for a
period of one year ending September 30, 1939.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT
Tue Waits Hovss,

August 12, 1938.

[No. 7954]

U.S. GOVERNMENT PRINTING OFFICE: 1938

```

Roosevelt, exempts John H. Thomas from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending September 30, 1939, under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a). The recital identifies John H. Thomas as a person who had been exempted for a period one year ending September 30, 1938, by Executive Order No. 7637 of June 19, 1937; that earlier exemption is a separate instrument, and nothing in it affects when this one ends. The held text is the American Presidency Project web transcription (APP node 362896, Common Crawl capture 2024-12-02T23:08:22Z); its page record in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl names Executive Order 7912, the date agrees with the text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file. The order is one whereas paragraph and one operative paragraph, so it is one clause. The exemption ends by its own terms on September 30, 1939; per research/readiness/owner_rulings/2026-10-21-until-and-further-exempt.md that day is recorded as the inclusive end, and the exclusive reading (the exemption stops when that day begins) is the alternative a reviewer should see. The OFR catalog search and the held-text name search both report zero hits for this order, so no revoking, superseding or extending instrument is recorded in the packet.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier ending is supported. Roosevelt, exempts David A. Brodie from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending July 31, 1939, under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a). The held text is the American Presidency Project web transcription (APP node 362898, Common Crawl capture 2024-11-06T09:24:37Z); its page record in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl names Executive Order 7914, the date agrees with the text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file. The order is one whereas paragraph and one operative paragraph, so it is one clause. The exemption ends by its own terms on July 31, 1939; per research/readiness/owner_rulings/2026-10-21-until-and-further-exempt.md that day is recorded as the inclusive end, and the exclusive reading (the exemption stops when that day begins) is the alternative a reviewer should see. The OFR catalog search and the held-text name search both report zero hits for this order, so no revoking, superseding or extending instrument is recorded in the packet.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier ending is supported. The order cites no condition other than its fixed period, the packet records no revocation or supersession of it (OFR catalog and U.S. Code and eCFR citation searches report zero hits), and a separation of David A. Roosevelt, exempts Ernest P. Rands from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending July 31, 1939, under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a). The held text is the American Presidency Project web transcription (APP node 362903, Common Crawl capture 2025-06-21T06:07:15Z); its page record in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl names Executive Order 7929, the date agrees with the text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file. The order is one whereas paragraph and one operative paragraph, so it is one clause. The exemption ends by its own terms on July 31, 1939; per research/readiness/owner_rulings/2026-10-21-until-and-further-exempt.md that day is recorded as the inclusive end, and the exclusive reading (the exemption stops when that day begins) is the alternative a reviewer should see. Roosevelt, exempts William H. Griffin from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending July 31, 1939, under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a). The recital identifies William H. Griffin as a person who had been exempted for a period of one year ending July 31, 1938, by Executive Order No. 7667 of July 17, 1937; that earlier exemption is a separate instrument, and nothing in it affects when this one ends. The held text is the American Presidency Project web transcription (APP node 362904, Common Crawl capture 2025-06-15T01:13:57Z); its page record in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl names Executive Order 7930, the date agrees with the text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file. The order is one whereas paragraph and one operative paragraph, so it is one clause. The exemption ends by its own terms on July 31, 1939; per research/readiness/owner_rulings/2026-10-21-until-and-further-exempt.md that day is recorded as the inclusive end, and the exclusive reading (the exemption stops when that day begins) is the alternative a reviewer should see. The OFR catalog search and the held-text name search both report zero hits for this order, so no revoking, superseding or extending instrument is recorded in the packet.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier ending is supported. The order cites no condition other than its fixed period, the packet records no revocation or supersession of it (OFR catalog and U.S. Code and eCFR citation searches report zero hits), and a separation of William H. Roosevelt, exempts Ulysses S. Shewmaker from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending September 30, 1939, under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a). The held text is the American Presidency Project web transcription (APP node 210530, Common Crawl capture 2025-09-14T05:28:18Z); its page record in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl names Executive Order 7932, the date agrees with the text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file. The order is one whereas paragraph and one operative paragraph, so it is one clause. The exemption ends by its own terms on September 30, 1939; per research/readiness/owner_rulings/2026-10-21-until-and-further-exempt.md that day is recorded as the inclusive end, and the exclusive reading (the exemption stops when that day begins) is the alternative a reviewer should see. The OFR catalog search and the held-text name search both report zero hits for this order, so no revoking, superseding or extending instrument is recorded in the packet.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier ending is supported. The order cites no condition other than its fixed period, the packet records no revocation or supersession of it (OFR catalog and U.S. Code and eCFR citation searches report zero hits), and a separation of Ulysses S. Shewmaker from service before September 30, 1939 would be a fact about the person rather than an act on the order and is not shown by any held source.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains no independent continuing command: it creates no report, regulation or duty, and the statute it cites is its authority, not a command of the order.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings are a fixed-period permission with no continuing consequence stated in the order or any held source; recorded as accrued during the period.\",\"scope_check\":\"The order names one person and has a single operative paragraph (the first paragraph only recites the finding of public interest). Roosevelt, exempts J. Humbird Smith from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending July 31, 1939, under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a). The held text is the American Presidency Project web transcription (APP node 362907, Common Crawl capture 2025-09-11T08:28:32Z); its page record in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl names Executive Order 7935, the date agrees with the text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file. The order is one whereas paragraph and one operative paragraph, so it is one clause. The exemption ends by its own terms on July 31, 1939; per research/readiness/owner_rulings/2026-10-21-until-and-further-exempt.md that day is recorded as the inclusive end, and the exclusive reading (the exemption stops when that day begins) is the alternative a reviewer should see. The OFR catalog search and the held-text name search both report zero hits for this order, so no revoking, superseding or extending instrument is recorded in the packet.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier ending is supported. Roosevelt, exempts Edward M. Weeks from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending August 31, 1939, under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a). The recital identifies Edward M. Weeks as a person who had been exempted for periods totaling two years by Executive Orders No. 7413 of July 10, 1936, and No. 7635 of June 16, 1937; that earlier exemption is a separate instrument, and nothing in it affects when this one ends. The held text is the American Presidency Project web transcription (APP node 362909, Common Crawl capture 2025-11-11T15:13:09Z); its page record in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl names Executive Order 7945, the date agrees with the text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file. The order is one whereas paragraph and one operative paragraph, so it is one clause. The exemption ends by its own terms on August 31, 1939; per research/readiness/owner_rulings/2026-10-21-until-and-further-exempt.md that day is recorded as the inclusive end, and the exclusive reading (the exemption stops when that day begins) is the alternative a reviewer should see. The packet records later extension order(s) No. 8231 (August 31, 1939); each is entered as a later event, and the machine search hits for this order (OFR catalog and held-text name search) are exactly those order(s).\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier ending is supported. The order cites no condition other than its fixed period, the packet records no revocation or supersession of it (OFR catalog and U.S. Code and eCFR citation searches report zero hits), and a separation of Edward M. Roosevelt, exempts Stephen W. Hamilton from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending August 31, 1939, under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a). The held text is the American Presidency Project web transcription (APP node 362911, Common Crawl capture 2025-10-17T11:49:20Z); its page record in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl names Executive Order 7949, the date agrees with the text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file. The order is one whereas paragraph and one operative paragraph, so it is one clause. The exemption ends by its own terms on August 31, 1939; per research/readiness/owner_rulings/2026-10-21-until-and-further-exempt.md that day is recorded as the inclusive end, and the exclusive reading (the exemption stops when that day begins) is the alternative a reviewer should see. The OFR catalog search and the held-text name search both report zero hits for this order, so no revoking, superseding or extending instrument is recorded in the packet.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier ending is supported. The order cites no condition other than its fixed period, the packet records no revocation or supersession of it (OFR catalog and U.S. Code and eCFR citation searches report zero hits), and a separation of Stephen W. Roosevelt, exempts William H. Egberts from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending September 30, 1939, under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a). The held text is the American Presidency Project web transcription (APP node 362912, Common Crawl capture 2025-05-19T22:34:59Z); its page record in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl names Executive Order 7950, the date agrees with the text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file. The order is one whereas paragraph and one operative paragraph, so it is one clause. The exemption ends by its own terms on September 30, 1939; per research/readiness/owner_rulings/2026-10-21-until-and-further-exempt.md that day is recorded as the inclusive end, and the exclusive reading (the exemption stops when that day begins) is the alternative a reviewer should see. Roosevelt, exempts Frank S. Smith from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending September 30, 1939, under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a). The held text is the American Presidency Project web transcription (APP node 362913, Common Crawl capture 2025-09-16T21:00:29Z); its page record in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl names Executive Order 7954, the date agrees with the text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file. The order is one whereas paragraph and one operative paragraph, so it is one clause. The exemption ends by its own terms on September 30, 1939; per research/readiness/owner_rulings/2026-10-21-until-and-further-exempt.md that day is recorded as the inclusive end, and the exclusive reading (the exemption stops when that day begins) is the alternative a reviewer should see. The OFR catalog search and the held-text name search both report zero hits for this order, so no revoking, superseding or extending instrument is recorded in the packet.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier ending is supported. The order cites no condition other than its fixed period, the packet records no revocation or supersession of it (OFR catalog and U.S. Code and eCFR citation searches report zero hits), and a separation of Frank S. Roosevelt, exempts Joseph H. Wheat from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending September 30, 1939, under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a). The held text is the American Presidency Project web transcription (APP node 362916, Common Crawl capture 2025-12-07T13:25:42Z); its page record in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl names Executive Order 7973, the date agrees with the text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file. The order is one whereas paragraph and one operative paragraph, so it is one clause. The exemption ends by its own terms on September 30, 1939; per research/readiness/owner_rulings/2026-10-21-until-and-further-exempt.md that day is recorded as the inclusive end, and the exclusive reading (the exemption stops when that day begins) is the alternative a reviewer should see. The OFR catalog search and the held-text name search both report zero hits for this order, so no revoking, superseding or extending instrument is recorded in the packet.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier ending is supported. The order cites no condition other than its fixed period, the packet records no revocation or supersession of it (OFR catalog and U.S. Code and eCFR citation searches report zero hits), and a separation of Joseph H. Roosevelt, exempts Alfred R. Gould from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending October 31, 1939, under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a). The held text is the American Presidency Project web transcription (APP node 210554, Common Crawl capture 2025-12-06T11:47:27Z); its page record in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl names Executive Order 7974, the date agrees with the text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file. The order is one whereas paragraph and one operative paragraph, so it is one clause. The exemption ends by its own terms on October 31, 1939; per research/readiness/owner_rulings/2026-10-21-until-and-further-exempt.md that day is recorded as the inclusive end, and the exclusive reading (the exemption stops when that day begins) is the alternative a reviewer should see. Roosevelt, exempts George Middleton from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending September 30, 1939, under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a). The held text is the American Presidency Project web transcription (APP node 210557, Common Crawl capture 2024-02-23T01:47:48Z); its page record in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl names Executive Order 7978, the date agrees with the text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file. The order is one whereas paragraph and one operative paragraph, so it is one clause. The exemption ends by its own terms on September 30, 1939; per research/readiness/owner_rulings/2026-10-21-until-and-further-exempt.md that day is recorded as the inclusive end, and the exclusive reading (the exemption stops when that day begins) is the alternative a reviewer should see. The packet records later extension order(s) No. 8217 (August 7, 1939); each is entered as a later event, and the machine search hits for this order (OFR catalog and held-text name search) are exactly those order(s).\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier ending is supported. Roosevelt, exempts Herman G. A. Brauer from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending September 30, 1939, under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a). The held text is the American Presidency Project web transcription (APP node 362917, Common Crawl capture 2024-03-04T18:50:46Z); its page record in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl names Executive Order 7982, the date agrees with the text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file. The order is one whereas paragraph and one operative paragraph, so it is one clause. The exemption ends by its own terms on September 30, 1939; per research/readiness/owner_rulings/2026-10-21-until-and-further-exempt.md that day is recorded as the inclusive end, and the exclusive reading (the exemption stops when that day begins) is the alternative a reviewer should see. Roosevelt, exempts Miss Ada Tanner from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending October 31, 1939, under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a). The held text is the American Presidency Project web transcription (APP node 362919, Common Crawl capture 2025-09-15T20:25:26Z); its page record in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl names Executive Order 7988, the date agrees with the text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file. The order is one whereas paragraph and one operative paragraph, so it is one clause. The exemption ends by its own terms on October 31, 1939; per research/readiness/owner_rulings/2026-10-21-until-and-further-exempt.md that day is recorded as the inclusive end, and the exclusive reading (the exemption stops when that day begins) is the alternative a reviewer should see. The OFR catalog search and the held-text name search both report zero hits for this order, so no revoking, superseding or extending instrument is recorded in the packet.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier ending is supported. The order cites no condition other than its fixed period, the packet records no revocation or supersession of it (OFR catalog and U.S. Code and eCFR citation searches report zero hits), and a separation of Miss Ada Tanner from service before October 31, 1939 would be a fact about the person rather than an act on the order and is not shown by any held source.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains no independent continuing command: it creates no report, regulation or duty, and the statute it cites is its authority, not a command of the order.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings are a fixed-period permission with no continuing consequence stated in the order or any held source; recorded as accrued during the period.\",\"scope_check\":\"The order names one person and has a single operative paragraph (the first paragraph only recites the finding of public interest). One clause covers the whole order; the clause span in the dossier is the full text from the whereas paragraph through the signature block.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:7988#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:7988#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"f9fade7ef6011c2bdf2481201ba87e6e7c4184d31af5440634056af7b20f130b","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7988-exemption-miss-ada-tanner-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","sha256":"f9fade7ef6011c2bdf2481201ba87e6e7c4184d31af5440634056af7b20f130b","state":"match"},"sha256":"07c035b1abfac1ac80f1a8a294756aec93dabe622cc59ca218e510fcebfe7795","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7988-exemption-miss-ada-tanner-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","text_sha256":"f9fade7ef6011c2bdf2481201ba87e6e7c4184d31af5440634056af7b20f130b"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"f9fade7ef6011c2bdf2481201ba87e6e7c4184d31af5440634056af7b20f130b","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7988-exemption-miss-ada-tanner-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","sha256":"f9fade7ef6011c2bdf2481201ba87e6e7c4184d31af5440634056af7b20f130b","state":"match"},"sha256":"07c035b1abfac1ac80f1a8a294756aec93dabe622cc59ca218e510fcebfe7795","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7988-exemption-miss-ada-tanner-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","text_sha256":"f9fade7ef6011c2bdf2481201ba87e6e7c4184d31af5440634056af7b20f130b"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"f9fade7ef6011c2bdf2481201ba87e6e7c4184d31af5440634056af7b20f130b","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7988-exemption-miss-ada-tanner-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","sha256":"f9fade7ef6011c2bdf2481201ba87e6e7c4184d31af5440634056af7b20f130b","state":"match"},"sha256":"07c035b1abfac1ac80f1a8a294756aec93dabe622cc59ca218e510fcebfe7795","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7988-exemption-miss-ada-tanner-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age Roosevelt, exempts Francis T. Leahy from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending October 31, 1939, under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a). The held text is the American Presidency Project web transcription (APP node 362920, Common Crawl capture 2025-01-24T04:25:02Z); its page record in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl names Executive Order 7989, the date agrees with the text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file. The order is one whereas paragraph and one operative paragraph, so it is one clause. The exemption ends by its own terms on October 31, 1939; per research/readiness/owner_rulings/2026-10-21-until-and-further-exempt.md that day is recorded as the inclusive end, and the exclusive reading (the exemption stops when that day begins) is the alternative a reviewer should see. The OFR catalog search and the held-text name search both report zero hits for this order, so no revoking, superseding or extending instrument is recorded in the packet.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier ending is supported. The order cites no condition other than its fixed period, the packet records no revocation or supersession of it (OFR catalog and U.S. Code and eCFR citation searches report zero hits), and a separation of Francis T. Leahy from service before October 31, 1939 would be a fact about the person rather than an act on the order and is not shown by any held source.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains no independent continuing command: it creates no report, regulation or duty, and the statute it cites is its authority, not a command of the order.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings are a fixed-period permission with no continuing consequence stated in the order or any held source; recorded as accrued during the period.\",\"scope_check\":\"The order names one person and has a single operative paragraph (the first paragraph only recites the finding of public interest). Roosevelt, exempts P. Julian Latham from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending November 30, 1939, under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a). The held text is the American Presidency Project web transcription (APP node 362922, Common Crawl capture 2024-04-20T02:40:43Z); its page record in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl names Executive Order 7991, the date agrees with the text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file. The order is one whereas paragraph and one operative paragraph, so it is one clause. The exemption ends by its own terms on November 30, 1939; per research/readiness/owner_rulings/2026-10-21-until-and-further-exempt.md that day is recorded as the inclusive end, and the exclusive reading (the exemption stops when that day begins) is the alternative a reviewer should see. The OFR catalog search and the held-text name search both report zero hits for this order, so no revoking, superseding or extending instrument is recorded in the packet.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier ending is supported. The order cites no condition other than its fixed period, the packet records no revocation or supersession of it (OFR catalog and U.S. Code and eCFR citation searches report zero hits), and a separation of P. Julian Latham from service before November 30, 1939 would be a fact about the person rather than an act on the order and is not shown by any held source.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains no independent continuing command: it creates no report, regulation or duty, and the statute it cites is its authority, not a command of the order.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings are a fixed-period permission with no continuing consequence stated in the order or any held source; recorded as accrued during the period.\",\"scope_check\":\"The order names one person and has a single operative paragraph (the first paragraph only recites the finding of public interest). One clause covers the whole order; the clause span in the dossier is the full text from the whereas paragraph through the signature The order is one whereas paragraph and one operative paragraph, so it is one clause. Roosevelt, exempts Wallace Streater from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending November 30, 1939, under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a). The held text is the American Presidency Project web transcription (APP node 362923, Common Crawl capture 2025-10-13T22:59:46Z); its page record in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl names Executive Order 7996, the date agrees with the text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file. The order is one whereas paragraph and one operative paragraph, so it is one clause. The exemption ends by its own terms on November 30, 1939; per research/readiness/owner_rulings/2026-10-21-until-and-further-exempt.md that day is recorded as the inclusive end, and the exclusive reading (the exemption stops when that day begins) is the alternative a reviewer should see. The OFR catalog search and the held-text name search both report zero hits for this order, so no revoking, superseding or extending instrument is recorded in the packet.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier ending is supported. Roosevelt, exempts Harry Peale from compulsory retirement for age for a period of six months ending July 31, 1939, under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a). The held text is the American Presidency Project web transcription (APP node 362925, Wayback Machine capture 2023-09-06T11:18:42Z); its page record in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl names Executive Order 8002, the date agrees with the text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file. The order is one whereas paragraph and one operative paragraph, so it is one clause. The exemption ends by its own terms on July 31, 1939; per research/readiness/owner_rulings/2026-10-21-until-and-further-exempt.md that day is recorded as the inclusive end, and the exclusive reading (the exemption stops when that day begins) is the alternative a reviewer should see. Chase from compulsory retirement for age \"for a period of one year ending November 30, 1939\", under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404). I read the registered American Presidency Project transcription in full, confirmed its identity against the Common Crawl capture record (number, date, subject heading and text hash agree), and confirmed that it contains one operative command. The clause ended by its own stated period on November 30, 1939, recorded inclusive with the exclusive reading disclosed. Later extension orders are recorded as later events and do not end this order's clause. The text is a transcription (grade mirror_transcription) and the original is not yet held; replace and re-check quotes when an official copy is retrieved.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Candidate earlier ends tested: express revocation, amendment or supersession by a later order; repeal of section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 before November 30, 1939; and the employee's separation. A repeat search by the producer of all held order texts for this order's number (the forms \\\"No. 8011\\\" and \\\"Executive Order(s) ... 8011\\\") found only these held order texts citing this order by number, each read in full: EO 8287; it is a further exemption of the same employee and does not revoke, amend or supersede this order. The packet's Federal Register disposition-note search (checked 2026-10-19) reported 1 hit for this order (the notes on EO 8287). The person-name search over held order texts (checked 2026-10-28) reported 1 hit (the same extension order). The packet's U.S. Code and eCFR searches (checked 2026-10-17) found no hit for this order. No held source shows a repeal or change of section 204 before November 30, 1939, and a general statute that did not name this order would be only a disclosed candidate under the owner ruling on unnamed instruments, not an end. Baker from compulsory retirement for age \"for a period of one year ending December 31, 1939\", under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404). I read the registered American Presidency Project transcription in full, confirmed its identity against the Common Crawl capture record (number, date, subject heading and text hash agree), and confirmed that it contains one operative command. The clause ended by its own stated period on December 31, 1939, recorded inclusive with the exclusive reading disclosed. No later instrument affecting this order was found in the held corpus. The text is a transcription (grade mirror_transcription) and the original is not yet held; replace and re-check quotes when an official copy is retrieved.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Candidate earlier ends tested: express revocation, amendment or supersession by a later order; repeal of section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 before December 31, 1939; and the employee's separation. A repeat search by the producer of all held order texts for this order's number (the forms \\\"No. 8024\\\" and \\\"Executive Order(s) ... 8024\\\") found no held order text citing this order by number. The packet's Federal Register disposition-note search (checked 2026-10-19) reported 0 hits for this order. The person-name search over held order texts (checked 2026-10-28) reported 0 hits. The packet's U.S. Code and eCFR searches (checked 2026-10-17) found no hit for this order. No held source shows a repeal or change of section 204 before December 31, 1939, and a general statute that did not name this order would be only a disclosed candidate under the owner ruling on unnamed instruments, not an end. The held record contains no separation or death date for the employee and none is needed: the permission was stated for a fixed period, so the stated end is the earliest end the held evidence supports.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains a single operative command, the exemption itself; the recital is the President's stated judgment on which the exemption rests and is not a separate command. The order directs no report, appointment, payment or other act by any official and states no condition, so no independent command survives its stated end.\",\"savings_check\":\"Tested whether any right, duty, proceeding or transition survived the stated end: the order contains none, and service already performed under the exemption is the only accrued effect. The savings source is the order's own text.\",\"scope_check\":\"The command covers one named employee, Percy A. McIlvaine from compulsory retirement for age \"for a period of one year ending February 29, 1940\", under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404). I read the registered American Presidency Project transcription in full, confirmed its identity against the Common Crawl capture record (number, date, subject heading and text hash agree), and confirmed that it contains one operative command. The clause ended by its own stated period on February 29, 1940, recorded inclusive with the exclusive reading disclosed. No later instrument affecting this order was found in the held corpus. The text is a transcription (grade mirror_transcription) and the original is not yet held; replace and re-check quotes when an official copy is retrieved.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Candidate earlier ends tested: express revocation, amendment or supersession by a later order; repeal of section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 before February 29, 1940; and the employee's separation. A repeat search by the producer of all held order texts for this order's number (the forms \\\"No. 8025\\\" and \\\"Executive Order(s) ... 8025\\\") found no held order text citing this order by number. The packet's Federal Register disposition-note search (checked 2026-10-19) reported 0 hits for this order. The person-name search over held order texts (checked 2026-10-28) reported 0 hits. The packet's U.S. Code and eCFR searches (checked 2026-10-17) found no hit for this order. No held source shows a repeal or change of section 204 before February 29, 1940, and a general statute that did not name this order would be only a disclosed candidate under the owner ruling on unnamed instruments, not an end. The held record contains no separation or death date for the employee and none is needed: the permission was stated for a fixed period, so the stated end is the earliest end the held evidence supports.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains a single operative command, the exemption itself; the recital is the President's stated judgment on which the exemption rests and is not a separate command.
